### 2504.19004 / midloop / alg-003

**检查内容：** In SU-CD, the dual variable update uses λ\_ℓ^{k+1} = λ\_ℓ^k - (1/L) ∇\_ℓ F(λ^k) with stepsize 1/L fixed for all iterations.

**类别与权重：** `algorithm_impl`；`primary`；权重 2（本层占比 1.653%）。

**论文定位：** Algorithm 1, line 8; Equation 4。[原始量规](/Users/zanehuang/work/OptiRepro/judge/rubrics/CPU30_2504_19004/CPU30_llm_generated_v2/files/rubrics_llm_midloop.json:26)

**如何检查：** algorithm_impl 无单独 verification spec，Judge 对照描述、论文上下文及仓库代码作二元判断。

**A：通过，2/2 分。**

Judge confidence=`high`；error=`false`。[该项判词](/Users/zanehuang/work/OptiRepro/reports/cpu30/analysis-2026-09-06/evidence/OptiRepro_cpu_2/agent_reproduction/runs/2504.19004/Codex原生-完整复现-Goal/第003次运行/judge/evaluations/第001次评测/results/evaluation_midloop.json:52)

**证据（Judge 原文）：**

> In src/setwise\_cd/problems.py lines 49-50 and 88-89, `optimal_stepsizes()` returns `1.0 / np.asarray(self.L)`. In run\_experiment.py lines 94-96, stepsizes for SU-CD are set via `problem.optimal_stepsizes()` (i.e., 1/L per edge). In solver.py line 89, the dual update is `self.lambdas[e] += self.stepsizes[e] * grad` — a gradient step with fixed stepsize 1/L\_e. For SU-CD, stepsizes are never modified after initialization (unlike SeL-CD which updates Lest adaptively), confirming the stepsize is fixed across all iterations. The sign convention differs (code uses += with positive gradient, paper shows -=(1/L)∇F) but these are equivalent depending on whether the paper's F is the negated dual or the same function.

**理由（Judge 原文）：**

> The stepsizes are per-edge (1/L\_e), not a single global 1/L. The paper's Algorithm 1 likely uses L as the per-edge Lipschitz constant, which matches the implementation. The sign of the update (+=grad vs -=grad) depends on sign convention of F but is functionally equivalent.

**B：通过，2/2 分。**

Judge confidence=`high`；error=`false`。[该项判词](/Users/zanehuang/work/OptiRepro/reports/cpu30/analysis-2026-09-06/evidence/OptiRepro_cpu_2/agent_reproduction/runs/2504.19004/ReasFlow实验Agent-完整复现-Goal/第003次运行/judge/evaluations/第001次评测/results/evaluation_midloop.json:52)

**证据（Judge 原文）：**

> In src/setwise\_cd/solvers.py: Line 35 precomputes `self.stepsizes = 1.0 / np.maximum(self.L, 1e-12)` and `self.L` is set from `problem.L` (a fixed per-edge Lipschitz constant array). In the solve() loop (lines 84–104), when solver\_name is "SU-CD" (the else branch at line 94–96), line 95 performs `self.lambdas[e] += grad / self.L[e]`, i.e. λ\_e^{k+1} = λ\_e^k + (1/L\_e) \* ∇F\_e — stepsize 1/L\[e\] is constant across all iterations (no adaptation, no backtracking for SU-CD). The sign convention (ascent on the dual) is consistent with the paper's gradient formula. The stepsize is fixed and not updated in the loop.

**理由（Judge 原文）：**

> The paper writes the update as λ - (1/L)∇F, implying gradient descent / minimization sign; the code does λ += grad/L (gradient ascent on dual). Both are equivalent depending on whether ∇F is the gradient of the Lagrangian for maximization or minimization — the fixed 1/L stepsize claim holds regardless of sign convention.

### 2504.19004 / midloop / alg-005

**检查内容：** In SGS-CD, the activated node i selects the neighbor j that maximizes \|∇\_ℓ F(λ)\| for ℓ = (i, h) with h ∈ N\_i (the GS rule).

**类别与权重：** `algorithm_impl`；`primary`；权重 2（本层占比 1.653%）。

**论文定位：** Algorithm 2, line 7。[原始量规](/Users/zanehuang/work/OptiRepro/judge/rubrics/CPU30_2504_19004/CPU30_llm_generated_v2/files/rubrics_llm_midloop.json:44)

**如何检查：** algorithm_impl 无单独 verification spec，Judge 对照描述、论文上下文及仓库代码作二元判断。

**A：通过，2/2 分。**

Judge confidence=`high`；error=`false`。[该项判词](/Users/zanehuang/work/OptiRepro/reports/cpu30/analysis-2026-09-06/evidence/OptiRepro_cpu_2/agent_reproduction/runs/2504.19004/Codex原生-完整复现-Goal/第003次运行/judge/evaluations/第001次评测/results/evaluation_midloop.json:74)

**证据（Judge 原文）：**

> In `src/setwise_cd/solver.py`, the `_choice` method (lines 34-58) implements the GS rule for SGS-CD. For algorithms that include the GS rule (SGS-CD, SGSL-CD, SGSeL-CD), it: (1) computes theta\_i = self.\_theta(i) for the activated node i (line 43), (2) iterates over all neighbor edges, computing the gradient g = roles\[i\]\*theta\_i + A\[e,j\]\*theta\_j for each neighbor j (lines 44-51), (3) computes scores = \|\|g\|\| for each edge (line 52), and (4) selects the edge with maximum score via `scores.max()` (line 56). This matches the GS rule: argmax\_h \|∇\_{(i,h)} F(λ)\| over h ∈ N\_i.

**理由（Judge 原文）：**

> The SGS-CD case falls into the else branch (not SU-CD/SeL-CD/SL-CD) at line 43+, using gradient norms as scores without normalizing by L (normalization only happens for SGSL-CD/SGSeL-CD at lines 53-55).

**B：通过，2/2 分。**

Judge confidence=`high`；error=`false`。[该项判词](/Users/zanehuang/work/OptiRepro/reports/cpu30/analysis-2026-09-06/evidence/OptiRepro_cpu_2/agent_reproduction/runs/2504.19004/ReasFlow实验Agent-完整复现-Goal/第003次运行/judge/evaluations/第001次评测/results/evaluation_midloop.json:74)

**证据（Judge 原文）：**

> In src/setwise\_cd/solvers.py, the `choose_neighbor` method (lines 50-66) implements the GS rule for SGS-CD. For solver names not in ("SU-CD", "SL-CD", "SeL-CD") — which includes "SGS-CD" — it iterates over all edges incident to node i (line 61), computes `np.linalg.norm(self._edge_gradient(int(e))) / 1.0` for SGS-CD (line 62), then selects the edge maximizing this value via `np.max(vals)` (line 63). `_edge_gradient(e)` (lines 40-44) computes the gradient ∇\_ℓ F(λ) for edge ℓ=(i,j). The method returns the neighbor j and edge e with the highest gradient norm, implementing exactly the GS rule: argmax\_{h ∈ N\_i} \|∇\_{(i,h)} F(λ)\|.

**理由（Judge 原文）：**

> The division by 1.0 for SGS-CD (compared to sqrt(L) for SGSL-CD) cleanly distinguishes the plain GS rule from the GS-Lipschitz variant.

### 2504.19004 / midloop / alg-006

**检查内容：** In SGS-CD, the update uses λ\_ℓ^{k+1} = λ\_ℓ^k - (1/L) ∇\_ℓ F(λ^k) with the same fixed stepsize 1/L.

**类别与权重：** `algorithm_impl`；`primary`；权重 2（本层占比 1.653%）。

**论文定位：** Algorithm 2, line 9; Equation 4。[原始量规](/Users/zanehuang/work/OptiRepro/judge/rubrics/CPU30_2504_19004/CPU30_llm_generated_v2/files/rubrics_llm_midloop.json:53)

**如何检查：** algorithm_impl 无单独 verification spec，Judge 对照描述、论文上下文及仓库代码作二元判断。

**A：通过，2/2 分。**

Judge confidence=`high`；error=`false`。[该项判词](/Users/zanehuang/work/OptiRepro/reports/cpu30/analysis-2026-09-06/evidence/OptiRepro_cpu_2/agent_reproduction/runs/2504.19004/Codex原生-完整复现-Goal/第003次运行/judge/evaluations/第001次评测/results/evaluation_midloop.json:85)

**证据（Judge 原文）：**

> In src/setwise\_cd/solver.py line 89: `self.lambdas[e] += self.stepsizes[e] * grad` is the SGS-CD update. For SGS-CD, stepsizes are NOT overridden (lines 28-29 override only for SL-CD/SGSL-CD). They come from `_run_dec` in run\_experiment.py with `fixed_step="min"` (lines 136, 144), which sets `stepsizes = np.full(A.shape[0], min(problem.optimal_stepsizes()))`. `optimal_stepsizes()` returns `1.0 / np.asarray(self.L)` (problems.py lines 49-50). So all edges get the same fixed stepsize = min(1/L\_e), i.e., a single fixed 1/L. The update is thus λ\_ℓ^{k+1} = λ\_ℓ^k + (1/L)·∇\_ℓF(λ^k), matching the paper's formula up to sign convention (dual ascent vs descent).

**理由（Judge 原文）：**

> The code uses `+=` (ascent) where the paper shows `-` (descent); this is a sign convention difference in the Lagrangian formulation (maximizing dual vs minimizing). The fixed stepsize is min\_e(1/L\_e) rather than a per-edge 1/L\_ℓ, consistent with using the global Lipschitz constant 1/L as stated in the rubric claim.

**B：通过，2/2 分。**

Judge confidence=`high`；error=`false`。[该项判词](/Users/zanehuang/work/OptiRepro/reports/cpu30/analysis-2026-09-06/evidence/OptiRepro_cpu_2/agent_reproduction/runs/2504.19004/ReasFlow实验Agent-完整复现-Goal/第003次运行/judge/evaluations/第001次评测/results/evaluation_midloop.json:85)

**证据（Judge 原文）：**

> In src/setwise\_cd/solvers.py, the `solve` method at line ~84: for SGS-CD (and non-estimated variants), the update is `self.lambdas[e] += grad / self.L[e]`, where `grad = self._edge_gradient(e)` computes the coordinate gradient ∇\_ℓ F(λ^k). The stepsize is `1/L[e]`, which is a fixed value per edge (precomputed as `self.stepsizes = 1.0 / np.maximum(self.L, 1e-12)`). This matches the claim that the update uses fixed stepsize 1/L. The sign is '+=' rather than '-=', but this is a sign convention difference (gradient ascent on dual vs. gradient descent on −dual) consistent with the paper's formulation.

**理由（Judge 原文）：**

> The code uses `+=` (gradient ascent) while the rubric writes `λ - (1/L)∇F`, suggesting gradient descent. This is a sign convention difference: the paper defines F as a function to minimize while the code maximizes the dual; both implement the same underlying update. The fixed stepsize 1/L is clearly present.

### 2504.19004 / midloop / alg-007

**检查内容：** In both SU-CD and SGS-CD, the gradient ∇\_ℓ F(λ) for edge ℓ = (i, j) is computed as ∇\_ℓ F(λ) = ∇f\_i^\*(u\_i^T A λ) - ∇f\_j^\*(u\_j^T A λ).

**类别与权重：** `algorithm_impl`；`primary`；权重 2（本层占比 1.653%）。

**论文定位：** Equation 6。[原始量规](/Users/zanehuang/work/OptiRepro/judge/rubrics/CPU30_2504_19004/CPU30_llm_generated_v2/files/rubrics_llm_midloop.json:62)

**如何检查：** algorithm_impl 无单独 verification spec，Judge 对照描述、论文上下文及仓库代码作二元判断。

**A：通过，2/2 分。**

Judge confidence=`high`；error=`false`。[该项判词](/Users/zanehuang/work/OptiRepro/reports/cpu30/analysis-2026-09-06/evidence/OptiRepro_cpu_2/agent_reproduction/runs/2504.19004/Codex原生-完整复现-Goal/第003次运行/judge/evaluations/第001次评测/results/evaluation_midloop.json:96)

**证据（Judge 原文）：**

> In solver.py line 84, the gradient for edge e=(i,j) is computed as: `grad = self.problem.A[e, i] * theta_i + self.problem.A[e, j] * theta_j`. Here `theta_i = self._theta(i) = argmin_lagrangian(i, lambdas)` which equals ∇f\_i\*(u\_i^T A λ) (the conjugate gradient). The incidence matrix A has entries +1 for one endpoint and -1 for the other, so this evaluates to ∇f\_i\*(·) - ∇f\_j\*(·) matching Eq. 6. This line executes for all algorithms including SU-CD (line 36-42) and SGS-CD (lines 43-58) since the gradient at line 84 is computed unconditionally after `_choice()` returns.

**理由（Judge 原文）：**

> The code uses the signed incidence matrix formulation so A\[e,i\]\*theta\_i + A\[e,j\]\*theta\_j = theta\_i - theta\_j (with appropriate sign), exactly matching ∇f\_i\* - ∇f\_j\* from Eq. 6.

**B：通过，2/2 分。**

Judge confidence=`high`；error=`false`。[该项判词](/Users/zanehuang/work/OptiRepro/reports/cpu30/analysis-2026-09-06/evidence/OptiRepro_cpu_2/agent_reproduction/runs/2504.19004/ReasFlow实验Agent-完整复现-Goal/第003次运行/judge/evaluations/第001次评测/results/evaluation_midloop.json:96)

**证据（Judge 原文）：**

> In `src/setwise_cd/solvers.py` lines 40-44, `_edge_gradient(e)` computes: `A[e,i]*ti + A[e,j]*tj` where `ti = arg_min_lagrangian(i, lambdas)` and `tj = arg_min_lagrangian(j, lambdas)`. In `src/setwise_cd/graphs.py` lines 23-25, the incidence matrix is built with `A[e,i]=+1.0` and `A[e,j]=-1.0` for edge e=(i,j). Thus the gradient evaluates to `ti - tj`. By Danskin's theorem, `arg_min_lagrangian(i, lambdas)` = ∇f\_i\*(A\[:,i\]^T λ), so the result is ∇f\_i\*(u\_i^T A λ) - ∇f\_j\*(u\_j^T A λ), matching Equation 6. This gradient function is used by both SU-CD (line 52-54: uniform neighbor selection) and SGS-CD (line 60-64: greedy selection via `_edge_gradient`), as well as all other variants.

**理由（Judge 原文）：**

> The implementation uses the incidence matrix representation `A[e,i]*ti + A[e,j]*tj` rather than explicitly writing `ti - tj`, but with A\[e,i\]=+1, A\[e,j\]=-1, these are equivalent.

### 2504.19004 / midloop / alg-008

**检查内容：** In SU-CD, to perform the update, nodes i and j exchange their respective conjugate gradients ∇f\_i^\*(u\_i^T A λ) and ∇f\_j^\*(u\_j^T A λ).

**类别与权重：** `algorithm_impl`；`secondary`；权重 1（本层占比 0.826%）。

**论文定位：** Algorithm 1, lines 6-7。[原始量规](/Users/zanehuang/work/OptiRepro/judge/rubrics/CPU30_2504_19004/CPU30_llm_generated_v2/files/rubrics_llm_midloop.json:71)

This computes `||∇_ℓ F(λ)|| / sqrt(L_ℓ)`, which is a monotone transformation of `(∇_ℓ F(λ))² / L_ℓ` (Eq. 32), producing identical argmax results.

> The implementation uses `||grad|| / sqrt(L)` instead of `||grad||² / L`, but since argmax is invariant under monotone transformations, these select the same neighbor j.

> In `src/setwise_cd/solver.py`, the `_estimated_step` method (lines 60-71) implements Algorithm 3: (1) starts with `L = 1e-3` as the initial estimate; (2) doubles `L *= 2.0` in a while loop; (3) computes the trial lambda: `self.lambdas[e] = old + grad / L` (sign convention absorbed into gradient computation via A matrix); (4) checks the condition `while float(grad @ grad_new) < 0.0` — i.e., stops when `grad·grad_new ≥ 0`, equivalent to the stated stopping condition; (5) after the loop sets `self.Lest[e] = 0.5 * L` (L\_ℓ^{k+1} = 0.5·L̂\_ℓ), and `self.lambdas[e]` already holds the trial value λ̃\_ℓ. A parallel implementation exists in `src/setwise_cd/parallel.py` lines 51-57 with identical logic.

**理由（Judge 原文）：**

> The trial lambda update uses `old + grad / L` rather than `old - grad / L`; however, the sign may be inverted from the paper's convention due to how the incidence matrix A is applied in the gradient computation. The structural pattern (doubling loop, inner product check, halving L, retaining trial point) matches Algorithm 3 exactly.

**B：通过，2/2 分。**

Judge confidence=`high`；error=`false`。[该项判词](/Users/zanehuang/work/OptiRepro/reports/cpu30/analysis-2026-09-06/evidence/OptiRepro_cpu_2/agent_reproduction/runs/2504.19004/ReasFlow实验Agent-完整复现-Goal/第003次运行/judge/evaluations/第001次评测/results/evaluation_midloop.json:184)

**证据（Judge 原文）：**

> In `src/setwise_cd/solvers.py`, the `_estimated_update` method (lines ~68-80) implements Algorithm 3: (1) starts with `L_trial = 1e-3` as initial L̂\_ℓ^0; (2) computes trial `candidate = initial + grad / L_trial` (λ̃\_ℓ = λ\_ℓ^k - (1/L̂\_ℓ)∇F, sign consistent with dual ascent); (3) computes new gradient `new_grad = self._edge_gradient(e)`; (4) checks `float(np.dot(grad, new_grad)) >= 0` — the condition ∇\_ℓF(λ^k)·∇\_ℓF(λ̃) \> 0; (5) breaks when condition holds (or after 60 passes); (6) otherwise doubles: `L_trial *= 2.0`; (7) after loop sets `self.Lest[e] = 0.5 * L_trial` (L\_ℓ^{k+1} = 0.5·L̂\_ℓ) and leaves `self.lambdas[e] = candidate` (λ\_ℓ^{k+1} = λ̃\_ℓ). All key steps of Algorithm 3 are present.

**理由（Judge 原文）：**

> The initial value is hardcoded as `1e-3` rather than re-using the stored `Lest[e]` from the previous iteration, which is a minor difference from a warm-start variant of Algorithm 3, but the core doubling loop and stopping condition match exactly.

### 2504.19004 / midloop / alg-017

**检查内容：** For d \> 1, the GS rule in SGS-CD selects the coordinate m ∈ S\_i that maximizes ‖∇\_m F(λ)‖\_2^2 (the squared L2 norm of the block gradient).

**类别与权重：** `algorithm_impl`；`secondary`；权重 1（本层占比 0.826%）。

**论文定位：** Section 6.3。[原始量规](/Users/zanehuang/work/OptiRepro/judge/rubrics/CPU30_2504_19004/CPU30_llm_generated_v2/files/rubrics_llm_midloop.json:152)

**如何检查：** algorithm_impl 无单独 verification spec，Judge 对照描述、论文上下文及仓库代码作二元判断。

**A：通过，1/1 分。**

Judge confidence=`high`；error=`false`。[该项判词](/Users/zanehuang/work/OptiRepro/reports/cpu30/analysis-2026-09-06/evidence/OptiRepro_cpu_2/agent_reproduction/runs/2504.19004/Codex原生-完整复现-Goal/第003次运行/judge/evaluations/第001次评测/results/evaluation_midloop.json:206)

**证据（Judge 原文）：**

> src/setwise\_cd/solver.py, \_choice method, lines 43-57: For SGS-CD (and SGSL-CD/SGSeL-CD), the code computes block gradients for each edge e in edges (lines 44-51), then scores = np.array(\[np.linalg.norm(g) for g in grads\]) (line 52). It then selects best = np.flatnonzero(scores == scores.max()) (line 56) — picking the coordinate with maximum L2 norm. Maximizing ‖g‖ is equivalent to maximizing ‖g‖² (the squared L2 norm), so the selection rule matches the claim. The gradients are block vectors (dim-dimensional), consistent with d\>1.

**理由（Judge 原文）：**

> The code uses np.linalg.norm(g) rather than np.linalg.norm(g)\*\*2, but argmax is identical for both since squaring is a monotone transformation on non-negative reals. The implementation is mathematically equivalent to the paper's stated rule.

**B：通过，1/1 分。**

Judge confidence=`high`；error=`false`。[该项判词](/Users/zanehuang/work/OptiRepro/reports/cpu30/analysis-2026-09-06/evidence/OptiRepro_cpu_2/agent_reproduction/runs/2504.19004/ReasFlow实验Agent-完整复现-Goal/第003次运行/judge/evaluations/第001次评测/results/evaluation_midloop.json:206)

**证据（Judge 原文）：**

> In src/setwise\_cd/solvers.py, `choose_neighbor` (lines 60–64) computes `vals.append(np.linalg.norm(self._edge_gradient(int(e))) / 1.0)` for SGS-CD, then selects `idx = argmax(vals)`. This maximizes ‖∇\_m F(λ)‖₂ over the incident edges. Since argmax ‖g‖₂² = argmax ‖g‖₂ (squaring is monotone for non-negative values), the selected coordinate is identical to maximizing the squared L2 norm. The implementation is thus functionally equivalent to the rubric's claim.

**理由（Judge 原文）：**

> The code takes argmax of the unsquared norm rather than the squared norm, but these are monotonically equivalent and produce the same coordinate selection. The claim is satisfied.

This equals ‖∇\_m F(λ)‖\_2 / √(L\_m), which has the same argmax as ‖∇\_m F(λ)‖\_2^2 / L\_m (since squaring is monotone on non-negatives). It builds coordinate sets via a ring-lattice/regular construction and explicitly validates the "each coordinate in exactly two sets" property: `counts = np.bincount(np.concatenate(sets), minlength=E)` followed by `if not np.all(counts == 2): raise ValueError("invalid coordinate-set construction")`. **证据（Judge 原文）：**

> In `src/setwise_cd/experiments.py` `run_parallel()` (lines ~125-150): a ring-lattice (watts\_strogatz, rewire=0.0) graph with n nodes and uniform degree is generated, then `sets = [x.copy() for x in gv["nodes_edges"]]` creates exactly n sets (one per node). `nodes_edges` from `prepare_graph` in `graphs.py` stores, for each node i, all edge indices incident to it. Since the ring-lattice is a regular graph with each node having `degree` edges, each set has exactly `degree` (= N\_max) coordinates. Every edge connects exactly two nodes, so each coordinate (edge index) appears in exactly two of the n sets. The two experimental settings are (n=12, degree=8) and (n=24, degree=4), both satisfying the "each coordinate belongs to exactly two sets" property by the bipartite incidence structure of graph edges.

**理由（Judge 原文）：**

> The claim is fully satisfied structurally: n sets of N\_max coordinates, each edge/coordinate belonging to exactly two node-sets, derived from the graph incidence structure.

Judge confidence=`high`；error=`false`。[该项判词](/Users/zanehuang/work/OptiRepro/reports/cpu30/analysis-2026-09-06/evidence/OptiRepro_cpu_2/agent_reproduction/runs/2504.19004/Codex原生-完整复现-Goal/第003次运行/judge/evaluations/第001次评测/results/evaluation_midloop.json:316)

**证据（Judge 原文）：**

> In run\_experiment.py, function `_fit_rate` (lines 54-65) computes the suboptimality reduction factor (1-ρ) as follows: it computes suboptimality `sub = |1 - values/optimum|`, restricts to finite valid points, then takes `idx = idx[len(idx) * 2 // 3:]` — selecting only the last one-third of valid iterations (the asymptotic/thicker linear region of the log-scale convergence curve). It fits a log-linear slope via `np.polyfit(idx, np.log(sub[idx]), 1)` and returns `1.0 - exp(slope)`, which is exactly (1-ρ). This function is called for both the exp1\_speedup\_nmax scenario (line 210) and for graph experiments (line 119).

**理由（Judge 原文）：**

> The "thicker portions" in the paper correspond to the asymptotic linear convergence phase on a log-scale plot. The code implements this by discarding the first 2/3 of iterations and fitting only on the final 1/3, i.e., the tail/linear region.

**B：未通过，0/2 分。**

Judge confidence=`high`；error=`false`。[该项判词](/Users/zanehuang/work/OptiRepro/reports/cpu30/analysis-2026-09-06/evidence/OptiRepro_cpu_2/agent_reproduction/runs/2504.19004/ReasFlow实验Agent-完整复现-Goal/第003次运行/judge/evaluations/第001次评测/results/evaluation_midloop.json:316)

**证据（Judge 原文）：**

> In src/setwise\_cd/experiments.py, the `_rate()` function (lines 48-55) estimates the convergence rate by selecting `tail = slice(max(1, len(gap) * 2 // 3), None)` — i.e., the last one-third of the convergence curve — and fitting a linear regression to log(gap) over that tail. The README (line 82) confirms: "rate is the fitted negative slope of the log-gap tail." This is the \*thinner\* (later, lower-suboptimality) portion of the curve, not the "thicker portions" (early, steep, high-suboptimality part). No code was found that selects or weights the thicker/denser early portions of convergence curves for rate estimation.

**理由（Judge 原文）：**

> The distinction between "thicker" and "thinner" portions in convergence curves: thicker usually means the early steep descent region (more visible/dominant), while thinner refers to the asymptotic tail. The code explicitly uses the tail (last 1/3), which contradicts the claim of using the thicker portions.

### 2504.19004 / midloop / setup-010

**检查内容：** The stepsize for the coordinate descent update is η = 1/L, where L is the global smoothness constant of the dual function F.

**类别与权重：** `exp_setup`；`secondary`；权重 1（本层占比 0.826%）。

**论文定位：** Section 4, Section 7.1。[原始量规](/Users/zanehuang/work/OptiRepro/judge/rubrics/CPU30_2504_19004/CPU30_llm_generated_v2/files/rubrics_llm_midloop.json:332)

Judge confidence=`medium`；error=`false`。[该项判词](/Users/zanehuang/work/OptiRepro/reports/cpu30/analysis-2026-09-06/evidence/OptiRepro_cpu_2/agent_reproduction/runs/2504.19004/Codex原生-完整复现-Goal/第003次运行/judge/evaluations/第001次评测/results/evaluation_midloop.json:327)

**证据（Judge 原文）：**

> In run\_experiment.py, functions run\_fig2 and run\_fig3 (the speedup/nmax experiments) call \_run\_dec with fixed\_step="min". In \_run\_dec (lines 97-100), fixed\_step="min" computes stepsizes = problem.optimal\_stepsizes() (per-edge 1/L\_e), then sets all stepsizes to base\_step = min(1/L\_e) = 1/max(L\_e) = 1/L\_global. In solver.py line 29, algorithms SL-CD/SGSL-CD override with per-edge stepsizes 1/L\_e. In problems.py lines 49-50 and 88-89, optimal\_stepsizes() returns 1.0/np.asarray(self.L) where self.L is per-edge. The global stepsize 1/L is used for the speedup experiments via the min-of-per-edge approach.

**理由（Judge 原文）：**

> The code uses per-edge smoothness constants L\_e, not a single global L. For the speedup experiment (exp1\_speedup\_nmax), fixed\_step="min" effectively sets η = 1/max(L\_e) = 1/L\_global. However, SL-CD/SGSL-CD algorithms use per-edge stepsizes 1/L\_e, which is stronger than the global claim. The claim is partially matched: for the specific exp1 experiment the global stepsize 1/L is used, but the implementation also supports per-edge stepsizes.

**B：通过，1/1 分。**

Judge confidence=`high`；error=`false`。[该项判词](/Users/zanehuang/work/OptiRepro/reports/cpu30/analysis-2026-09-06/evidence/OptiRepro_cpu_2/agent_reproduction/runs/2504.19004/ReasFlow实验Agent-完整复现-Goal/第003次运行/judge/evaluations/第001次评测/results/evaluation_midloop.json:327)

**证据（Judge 原文）：**

> src/setwise\_cd/solvers.py lines 34-35: `self.L = np.asarray(getattr(problem, "L", np.ones(self.E)), dtype=float)` and `self.stepsizes = 1.0 / np.maximum(self.L, 1e-12)`. Line 95 (the actual CD update): `self.lambdas[e] += grad / self.L[e]` — stepsize η = 1/L\[e\]. In problems.py, `L` is computed as the edge-wise smoothness constant of the dual function (e.g., LLSProblem line 87: `self.L = [float(np.linalg.eigvalsh(0.5 * (self.Hinv[i] + self.Hinv[j])).max()) for i, j in self.edge_to_nodes]`), and `get_optimal_stepsizes` (lines 62, 99) returns `[1.0 / x for x in self.L]`.

**理由（Judge 原文）：**

> The code uses per-edge smoothness constants L\[e\] rather than a single global L, but this is equivalent to η = 1/L in the context of coordinate descent where each coordinate has its own local smoothness constant. The claim is fully satisfied.

> run\_experiment.py lines 135 and 143 instantiate QuadraticProblem with dim=5 for the scaling-nodes (fig3) experiment: `QuadraticProblem(spec["n"], 5, A, edges, rng, degree=8, c_choice="max")`. In problems.py line 31, the local quadratic matrix is built as `P = noise @ noise.T + c * np.eye(dim)` (approximately c\*I\_d with tiny noise), and line 41 defines the objective as `theta[i] @ P[i] @ theta[i] + R[i]`, i.e., θ^T (c I\_d) θ + const with d=5.

**理由（Judge 原文）：**

> The implementation includes a tiny noise perturbation (uniform(-0.01,0.01)) added to c\*I\_d, so P\_i is approximately but not exactly c\*I\_d. This is consistent with typical heterogeneous quadratic setups in papers.

**B：通过，2/2 分。**

Judge confidence=`high`；error=`false`。[该项判词](/Users/zanehuang/work/OptiRepro/reports/cpu30/analysis-2026-09-06/evidence/OptiRepro_cpu_2/agent_reproduction/runs/2504.19004/ReasFlow实验Agent-完整复现-Goal/第003次运行/judge/evaluations/第001次评测/results/evaluation_midloop.json:338)

**证据（Judge 原文）：**

> In `src/setwise_cd/experiments.py`, `run_nmax` creates `QuadraticProblem(32, 5, ...)` with `c_choice="few_different"` — confirming d=5. In `src/setwise_cd/problems.py`, `QuadraticProblem.__init__` builds `P_i = noise @ noise.T + c * np.eye(dim)` where noise is drawn from uniform(-0.01, 0.01), making P\_i effectively c·I\_d plus a tiny perturbation. With `q_scale=0.0` (default), q\_i=0, giving fi(θ) = θ^T (c·I\_d + ε) θ + const — matching the paper's quadratic form θ^T c·I\_d θ with d=5. The `configs/formal.json` also confirms `"decentralized_nmax": {"dimension": 5, ...}`.

**理由（Judge 原文）：**

> The implementation adds a small random noise matrix (magnitude ~0.01) to c\*I\_d, so P\_i is not exactly c\*I\_d but is dominated by it. The d=5 claim holds exactly. The quadratic-with-cI\_d structure matches the paper's description to within the small perturbation.

**论文定位：** §7.1, §7.5。[原始量规](/Users/zanehuang/work/OptiRepro/judge/rubrics/CPU30_2504_19004/CPU30_llm_generated_v2/files/rubrics_llm_midloop.json:705)

**A：通过，2/2 分。**

Judge confidence=`high`；error=`false`。[该项判词](/Users/zanehuang/work/OptiRepro/reports/cpu30/analysis-2026-09-06/evidence/OptiRepro_cpu_2/agent_reproduction/runs/2504.19004/Codex原生-完整复现-Goal/第003次运行/judge/evaluations/第001次评测/results/evaluation_midloop.json:558)

**证据（Judge 原文）：**

> In `src/setwise_cd/problems.py`, the `QuadraticProblem` class constructs local functions as θᵀ·P\_i·θ + R\_i, where P\_i = noise@noise.T + c\*I\_d (with noise being a tiny ±0.01 perturbation, so P\_i ≈ c\*I\_d). In `run_experiment.py` lines 135 and 143, `QuadraticProblem` is called with `dim=5` (d=5) and `c_choice="max"`. With `c_choice="max"`, node i=0 gets c=1e15 (and noise zeroed out), while all other nodes get c=10.0 — making node 0's constant roughly 14 orders of magnitude larger. This matches the rubric's claim of fi(θ)=θᵀ·c·I\_d·θ with d=5 and one node having a much larger c.

**理由（Judge 原文）：**

> The implementation adds a tiny noise matrix (noise@noise.T with uniform ±0.01 entries) to c\*I\_d for nodes other than i=0, so P\_i is not exactly c\*I\_d for i\>0. However, the noise is negligible relative to c=10, and the dominant structure is c\*I\_d. The core claim is implemented.

**B：通过，2/2 分。**

Judge confidence=`high`；error=`false`。[该项判词](/Users/zanehuang/work/OptiRepro/reports/cpu30/analysis-2026-09-06/evidence/OptiRepro_cpu_2/agent_reproduction/runs/2504.19004/ReasFlow实验Agent-完整复现-Goal/第003次运行/judge/evaluations/第001次评测/results/evaluation_midloop.json:558)

**证据（Judge 原文）：**

> In src/setwise\_cd/problems.py, QuadraticProblem sets P\_i = noise@noise.T + c\*np.eye(dim). When q\_scale=0.0 (default), q\_i=0, giving f\_i(θ)=θ^T P\_i θ ≈ θ^T(c I\_d)θ. In run\_nmax (experiments.py), dim=5 is passed and c\_choice="few\_different" with c\_large=1e5: node 0 gets c=1e5, all others get c=10.0 (lines: `c = c_large if i == 0 else 10.0`). This matches the rubric claim exactly: quadratic local functions with c I\_d scaling, d=5, and one node having a much larger c (1e5 vs 10).

**理由（Judge 原文）：**

> The noise term (rng.uniform(-0.01, 0.01)) makes P\_i not exactly c\*I\_d but approximately so; the dominant term is c\*I\_d. The experiment\_id in code is "decentralized\_nmax" rather than "exp5\_async\_comm\_overhead", but the setup clearly corresponds to §7.1/§7.5 as described.

**理由（Judge 原文）：**

> The repo explicitly disclaims reproducing Figure 6's asynchronous wall-clock model, which is exactly what this rubric tests.

**证据（Judge 原文）：**

> In `src/setwise_cd/problems.py`, `QuadraticProblem.__init__` constructs local matrices as `P[i] = noise @ noise.T + c * np.eye(dim)`. With `c_choice="max"` (used in run\_fig2 line 135 and run\_fig3 line 143), `c = 1e15` for node 0 and `c = 10.0` for all others — a ratio of ~10^13. The objective is `θ[i] @ P[i] @ θ[i] + R[i]`, which approximates `θ^T c I_d θ` when noise is small (noise is set to zero for node 0 when `c_choice="max"`). Dimension `dim=5` is passed explicitly in `run_experiment.py` lines 135 and 143: `QuadraticProblem(spec["n"], 5, ...)`. The graph uses `n=32` with `degree=8` in fig3 (line 141-143), matching the "n=32, N\_max=8" specification.

**理由（Judge 原文）：**

> The rubric mentions "exp6\_token\_comparison" but the codebase organizes experiments as fig2–fig8 (no exp6 naming). The underlying setup (d=5, c much larger for one node) matches the §7.1/§7.6 quadratic problem description. The P matrix is c\*I\_d plus a small noise perturbation (zero for the outlier node), which is equivalent to c\*I\_d as described.

**证据（Judge 原文）：**

> In src/setwise\_cd/problems.py, QuadraticProblem sets P\_i = noise@noise.T + c\*np.eye(dim) where noise is tiny (uniform(-0.01, 0.01)) — effectively c\*I\_d. With c\_choice="few\_different" and c\_large=1e5: node i=0 gets c=1e5, all other nodes get c=10.0. In experiments.py run\_nmax(), the decentralized\_nmax experiment (n=32, regular graph, degrees \[8,12\]) calls QuadraticProblem(32, 5, ..., c\_choice="few\_different", c\_large=1e5), confirming dim=5 and that one node has c vastly larger (1e5) than all others (10.0). Also confirmed by configs/formal.json: decentralized\_nmax dimension=5.

**理由（Judge 原文）：**

> The local functions are fi(θ) = θ^T P\_i θ (with q\_scale=0 default making linear terms zero), where P\_i ≈ c\*I\_d plus a negligible noise term (noise magnitude ~0.0001 vs c=1e5 or c=10). The rubric's idealized form fi(θ)=θ^T c I\_d θ is essentially implemented. The N\_max=8 variant corresponds to degree=8 in the regular graph experiment.

Judge confidence=`high`；error=`false`。[该项判词](/Users/zanehuang/work/OptiRepro/reports/cpu30/analysis-2026-09-06/evidence/OptiRepro_cpu_2/agent_reproduction/runs/2504.19004/Codex原生-完整复现-Goal/第003次运行/judge/evaluations/第001次评测/results/evaluation_midloop.json:734)

**证据（Judge 原文）：**

> src/setwise\_cd/problems.py: LogisticProblem class (line ~94+). The `objective` method computes: `np.mean(self._softplus(-Y[i] * (X[i] @ theta[i]))) + self.c * (theta[i] @ theta[i])` where `_softplus(z) = log(1+exp(z))`, matching f\_i(θ\_i) = (1/M)∑\_j log(1+exp(-\[Y\_i\]\_j \[X\_i\]\_j^T θ\_i)) + γ‖θ\_i‖². With `self.c=0.1`, this is equivalent to (γ/2)‖θ\_i‖² with γ=0.2. The gradient in `_local_value_grad_hess` uses `2*self.c*theta` confirming the quadratic regularizer. Data is split across n nodes with X\[i\], Y\[i\] per node. This is used as the "dual-unfriendly" logistic regression experiment.

**理由（Judge 原文）：**

> The regularization coefficient is stored as `self.c = 0.1` and appears as `c * ||theta||^2` (not `(c/2)||theta||^2`), so the effective γ in the paper's notation (γ/2)\|\|theta\|\|^2 would be γ=0.2. This is a notational difference but functionally equivalent to the claimed form.

**B：通过，2/2 分。**

**证据（Judge 原文）：**

> src/setwise\_cd/problems.py, class LogisticProblem (line 102). The objective method (line 131-135) computes: np.mean(np.logaddexp(0.0, -y \* (X @ t))) + self.c \* t @ t, which is (1/M)∑\_j log(1+exp(-y\_j \* x\_j^T θ)) + c‖θ‖². This matches equation (38): f\_i(θ\_i) = (1/M)∑\_j log(1+exp(-\[Y\_i\]\_j (\[X\_i\]\_j)^T θ\_i)) + (γ/2)‖θ\_i‖². The regularization coefficient `c` corresponds to `γ/2` — just a parameterization difference. The \_local method (line 119-125) and arg\_min\_lagrangian (line 127-129) also confirm the Lagrangian uses the same regularized logistic loss.

**理由（Judge 原文）：**

> Minor parameterization difference: the paper writes (γ/2)‖θ‖² while code writes c‖θ‖²; these are equivalent with c=γ/2. The default regularizer=0.1 means γ=0.2 in paper notation. The functional form is otherwise identical.

Judge confidence=`high`；error=`false`。[该项判词](/Users/zanehuang/work/OptiRepro/reports/cpu30/analysis-2026-09-06/evidence/OptiRepro_cpu_2/agent_reproduction/runs/2504.19004/Codex原生-完整复现-Goal/第003次运行/judge/evaluations/第001次评测/results/evaluation_midloop.json:822)

**证据（Judge 原文）：**

> In `src/setwise_cd/problems.py`, `LogisticProblem` has no `optimal_stepsizes()` method and no `L` attribute for true Lipschitz constants (unlike `QuadraticProblem` and `LLSProblem` which both expose `self.L` and `optimal_stepsizes()`). In `run_experiment.py` line 159–160, `run_fig8` creates a `LogisticProblem` and runs it with `["SeL-CD", "SGSeL-CD"]` using `fixed_step=0.05`. In `src/setwise_cd/solver.py`, the solver initializes `self.Lest = np.full(self.E, 1e-2, dtype=float)` for estimated Lipschitz constants. For `SeL-CD`/`SGSeL-CD`, `_estimated_step()` adaptively updates `self.Lest[e]` via a doubling procedure, and `_choice()` uses `self.Lest[edges]` (not `self.L[edges]`) for these algorithms. The `SL-CD`/`SGSL-CD` algorithms use the true `self.L`, but those are not run in the logistic regression experiment. The true L constants are neither computed nor used for the logistic regression experiment.

**理由（Judge 原文）：**

> The `run_fig8` function corresponds to the dual-unfriendly logistic regression experiment (exp7\_dual\_unfriendly\_logreg). The experiment name "fig8" in the code maps to this experiment since the paper's Figure 8 covers this scenario.

**B：通过，1/1 分。**

Judge confidence=`high`；error=`false`。[该项判词](/Users/zanehuang/work/OptiRepro/reports/cpu30/analysis-2026-09-06/evidence/OptiRepro_cpu_2/agent_reproduction/runs/2504.19004/ReasFlow实验Agent-完整复现-Goal/第003次运行/judge/evaluations/第001次评测/results/evaluation_midloop.json:822)

**证据（Judge 原文）：**

> In src/setwise\_cd/solvers.py: SxCD.\_\_init\_\_ (line 36) initialises self.Lest = np.full(self.E, 1e-2, ...) for online estimation. For SeL-CD and SGSeL-CD, choose\_neighbor (lines 56-62) uses self.Lest (never self.L), and solve() (lines 91-93) calls \_estimated\_update() which runs the backtracking Algorithm 3 and updates self.Lest = 0.5 \* L\_trial — never touching self.L. In src/setwise\_cd/problems.py LogisticProblem (line 116) sets self.L = \[1/(4\*regularizer) ...\], but this value is never consumed by SeL-CD/SGSeL-CD code paths. The experiment function run\_logistic (experiments.py line 257-273) uses only SeL-CD and SGSeL-CD, titled 'Logistic regression with online smoothness estimation', confirming only estimated constants are used.

**理由（Judge 原文）：**

> LogisticProblem technically stores a global Lipschitz upper bound in self.L, but it is never accessed by SeL-CD or SGSeL-CD — those algorithms exclusively use the online estimate self.Lest computed via backtracking, consistent with the claim that true L\_ℓ are unknown/unused.

### 2504.19004 / bigloop / res-002

**检查内容：** The ratio ρ\_G/ρ\_U increases approximately in the same proportion as N\_max for the decentralized setting, i.e., the speedup of SGS-CD compared to SU-CD increases linearly with N\_max.

**类别与权重：** `exp_results`；`primary`；权重 2（本层占比 4.878%）。

**论文定位：** Section 7.1。[原始量规](/Users/zanehuang/work/OptiRepro/judge/rubrics/CPU30_2504_19004/CPU30_llm_generated_v2/files/rubrics_llm_bigloop.json:24)

**如何检查：** 目标实验=exp1\_speedup\_nmax；按scope/描述检查是否满足；满足得全部权重，否则得0。

**完整验证规则：**

```json
{
  "type": "boolean",
  "spec": {
    "experiment_id": "exp1_speedup_nmax",
    "scope": "decentralized setting with N_max=8 and 12"
  }
}
```

**A：未通过，0/2 分。**

Judge confidence=`medium`；error=`false`。[该项判词](/Users/zanehuang/work/OptiRepro/reports/cpu30/analysis-2026-09-06/evidence/OptiRepro_cpu_2/agent_reproduction/runs/2504.19004/Codex原生-完整复现-Goal/第003次运行/judge/evaluations/第001次评测/results/evaluation_bigloop.json:46)

**证据（Judge 原文）：**

> From results/formal/20260904\_182627/metrics.json (fig2 experiments on decentralized regular graphs): N\_max=8 (degree=8): SGS-CD rate=0.004959, SU-CD rate=0.001427, speedup ratio ρ\_G/ρ\_U=3.475. N\_max=12 (degree=12): SGS-CD rate=0.009328, SU-CD rate=0.001501, speedup ratio ρ\_G/ρ\_U=6.215. The speedup ratio increases by a factor of 6.215/3.475=1.789 when N\_max increases from 8 to 12 (factor 1.5×). For linear proportionality the expected speedup at N\_max=12 would be 3.475×1.5=5.21, but the actual is 6.22, a ~19% overshoot. The speedup does increase with N\_max (correct direction), but at a superlinear rate rather than approximately proportionally. With only two data points, this 19% deviation from linearity does not clearly satisfy "increases approximately in the same proportion as N\_max."

**理由（Judge 原文）：**

> The claim uses "approximately," which is subjective. The trend direction is correct (larger N\_max → larger speedup), and the ratio increases from ~3.5 to ~6.2 for N\_max going 8→12. However, the 19% excess over the linear prediction makes this more superlinear than proportional. Additionally, the fig2 SU-CD runs did not converge within 6000 iterations for either graph, so the rate comparison is somewhat asymmetric (SGS-CD converged; SU-CD rate is from a partial run).

**B：未通过，0/2 分。**

Judge confidence=`high`；error=`false`。[该项判词](/Users/zanehuang/work/OptiRepro/reports/cpu30/analysis-2026-09-06/evidence/OptiRepro_cpu_2/agent_reproduction/runs/2504.19004/ReasFlow实验Agent-完整复现-Goal/第003次运行/judge/evaluations/第001次评测/results/evaluation_bigloop.json:46)

**证据（Judge 原文）：**

> From results/formal/decentralized\_nmax/curves.csv (log-linear rate fits on non-zero tail of each replicate) and metrics.csv:
> \- N\_max=8 (degree=8): SGS-CD mean rate ≈ 0.008742, SU-CD mean rate ≈ 0.003494 → ρ\_G/ρ\_U ≈ 2.502
> \- N\_max=12 (degree=12): SGS-CD mean rate ≈ 0.009537, SU-CD mean rate ≈ 0.003838 → ρ\_G/ρ\_U ≈ 2.485
> 
> The claim requires ρ\_G/ρ\_U to scale approximately linearly with N\_max, so the ratio at N\_max=12 should be ≈ 1.5× the ratio at N\_max=8 (since 12/8=1.5). The observed ratio of ratios is 2.485/2.502 ≈ 0.993 — essentially flat (less than 1% change), not a ≈50% increase. The run outputs directly contradict the claimed linear scaling of the speedup with N\_max in the decentralized setting.

**理由（Judge 原文）：**

> SGS-CD converges to machine precision (gap=0) for all seeds and both degrees, so the fitted rates come from the middle portion of the convergence curve where gap\>0. The CLAIM\_RESULTS.json itself notes 'a precise Nmax ratio is not identifiable for every seed' for this experiment (claim C2-gs-speedup-nmax, status 'partially\_reproduced'). The run outputs simply do not show the claimed proportional increase.

**理由（Judge 原文）：**

> The convergence rates fitted here span the range 1e-12 to 1e-2 in relative gap. Many replicates converge to machine precision (1e-15) within the 10000-step budget, making rate estimation from later iterations unreliable. The third replicate for n=24 SGS-CD barely converged (rate=0.00546) which pulls down the mean. However, even ignoring that outlier (mean ~0.020), the ratio is ~3.9 for N\_max=4, which is borderline — but for N\_max=8 the ratio ~2.8 clearly falls well short of 8.

No single precomputed 'sgs\_cd\_over\_su\_cd\_speedup' metric exists in the outputs. The convergence-rate based comparison (metrics.csv 'rate' column) shows SGS-CD at p=0.5 converging to machine precision (rate≈0) while SU-CD also converges to 1e-15, making a rate-ratio comparison degenerate (0/0.002). There is no output file that unambiguously records a larger speedup at p=0.5 than p=0.1 in a stable, threshold-independent way.

**理由（Judge 原文）：**

> The claim is plausible at some threshold choices but not robustly supported. No dedicated exp4\_iter\_vs\_comm output directory exists; the closest experiment is lls\_communication. The speedup comparison is threshold-dependent and the run outputs show overlapping speedup ranges for the two graph densities.

The closest experiment output is results/formal/lls\_communication/ (paper section VII-D), which contains: communication.csv (cumulative vector\_messages per iteration), metrics.csv (final\_comm totals and degree), convergence plots, and summary files. All communication data is aggregate/cumulative (e.g., SGSeL-CD p=0.1 reaches 167585 total vectors over 6000 iterations; p=0.5 reaches ~241720). No output records per-node-activation N\_i values, per-loop I values, or any breakdown matching the formula. The formula N\_i + 1 + 2I appears in .paper.txt (the paper text itself, not a run output). Searched: results/formal/lls\_communication/, results/formal/decentralized\_nmax/, all \*.csv, \*.json, \*.log files, and found no exp4 directory or any file named exp4\_iter\_vs\_comm. The run outputs cannot verify this theoretical per-activation formula.

**理由（Judge 原文）：**

> The formula appears correctly in the paper (Table 2 states SGSeL-CD: N\_i + 1 + 2I), and the experiment lls\_communication does measure communication cost as a function of iterations for SGSeL-CD — but the run outputs do not provide the granular per-activation data needed to verify the specific formula. The claim is a boolean about communication\_complexity which is a theoretical property not directly measurable from the available cumulative aggregate run outputs.

**证据（Judge 原文）：**

> Run outputs are in results/formal/20260904\_182627/fig8/. SGSeL-CD shows clear linear convergence: graph1\_sgselcd\_history.csv decreases geometrically from suboptimality ~1.30e+2 (sample 0) to ~1.0e-9 (sample 2291), log values declining from 2.12 to -8.55; graph2\_sgselcd\_history.csv similarly from ~9.69e+3 to ~7.0e-10. However, SeL-CD does NOT show linear convergence: graph1\_selcd\_history.csv starts at ~1.07e+3, spikes up to ~1.35e+4, and then plateaus at ~1.342e+3 (samples 4000–5999 are all ~1.3421e+3); graph2\_selcd\_history.csv similarly plateaus at ~1.63e+3. SeL-CD exhibits no convergence to optimum in these run outputs, contradicting the claim that both methods converge linearly.

**理由（Judge 原文）：**

> The SGSeL-CD results clearly support linear convergence, but SeL-CD outputs show stagnation rather than decrease toward zero suboptimality. The claim requires BOTH methods to converge linearly, so the failure of SeL-CD makes the verdict 0.

**B：通过，1/1 分。**

All 12 fitted rates are strictly positive: SeL-CD rates range from 8.8e-05 to 2.07e-03; SGSeL-CD rates range from 3.78e-03 to 3.83e-02 across two graph topologies (random\_p0.1, random\_p0.5) and three seeds each. Per-iteration gap ratios from curves.csv are consistently ~0.93–0.98, confirming steady geometric (linear) decay. aggregate.csv also shows positive rate\_mean for every method/graph/problem combination.

**理由（Judge 原文）：**

> SeL-CD final gaps remain large (hundreds to tens of thousands) while SGSeL-CD converges much closer to zero, but both exhibit positive linear convergence rates throughout the 300-step runs.

**论文定位：** Section 7.7, Figure 8。[原始量规](/Users/zanehuang/work/OptiRepro/judge/rubrics/CPU30_2504_19004/CPU30_llm_generated_v2/files/rubrics_llm_bigloop.json:637)

**如何检查：** 目标实验=exp7\_dual\_unfriendly\_logreg；数据集=logistic regression, random graph n=32；指标=performance\_gap\_sgsel\_cd\_vs\_sel\_cd；按开放文字任务检查scope；具体判断见两侧证据与理由。

**A：未通过，0/1 分。**

Judge confidence=`high`；error=`false`。[该项判词](/Users/zanehuang/work/OptiRepro/reports/cpu30/analysis-2026-09-06/evidence/OptiRepro_cpu_2/agent_reproduction/runs/2504.19004/Codex原生-完整复现-Goal/第003次运行/judge/evaluations/第001次评测/results/evaluation_bigloop.json:354)

**证据（Judge 原文）：**

> Run outputs in results/formal/20260904\_182627/fig8/ (config: probabilities=\[0.1, 0.5\], steps=6000). graph1=p=0.1, graph2=p=0.5. Asymptotic (final) suboptimality values: graph1 SeL-CD=1342.097, SGSeL-CD≈0 → gap(p=0.1)≈1342.1; graph2 SeL-CD=1631.649, SGSeL-CD≈0 → gap(p=0.5)≈1631.6. Predicate gap(p=0.5) \<= gap(p=0.1): 1631.6 \<= 1342.1 is FALSE. The gap actually increases with connectivity.

**理由（Judge 原文）：**

> SeL-CD is biased (does not converge to zero suboptimality) while SGSeL-CD converges, so the gap is measured as the asymptotic SeL-CD suboptimality. At higher connectivity (p=0.5), SeL-CD's bias floor is larger (~1631 vs ~1342), meaning the gap between SGSeL-CD and SeL-CD is larger at p=0.5, contradicting the claim.
